, where
\mathcal{H}^{q}
is the
p
-th cohomology sheaf of the complex of
L^{\bullet}_{\omega}
. Therefore,
E_{2}^{p,q}=0
if
q<m
. Note that
E_{3}^{p,q}
is given by

\frac{\mathrm{Ker}\,d_{2}:E^{p,q}\to E^{p+2,q-1}}{{\rm Im}d_{2}:E^{p-2,q+1}\to E^{p,q}},

thus is zero for
q<m
. By virtue of the same manner, we can show that
E_{\infty}^{p,q}=0
for
q<m
. Note that

F^{p}\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet}_{\omega})/F^{p+1}\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet}_{\omega})=E_{\infty}^{p,i-p}.

for any
p\in\{0,\ldots,i\}
. It follows that if
i<m
, they all vanish. Hence
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet}_{\omega})=0
for
i<m
. The lemma is proved.
∎

Proof of
12.2
.

We will establish a relative Dolbeaut complex as
Section

11.2
, but without boundary divisors. Let
\mathcal{X}:=X\times\mathbb{C}
and
\mathcal{L}:=p_{1}^{*}L
, where
p_{1}:\mathcal{X}\to X
is the projection map. Set
\tilde{\omega}:=zp_{1}^{*}\omega
, where
z
is the coordinate for the second factor
\mathbb{C}
of
X\times\mathbb{C}
. We define a morphism

\theta:\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C}}^{1}

defined by the composition

\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wedge\tilde{\omega}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\to\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C}}^{1}

where the second map is induced by the quotient
\Omega_{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\to\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C}}^{1}
. Then
\theta\wedge\theta=0
and we have a holomorphic (relative Dolbeaut) complex

{\rm Dol}(\mathcal{L},\theta):=\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\to}}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C}}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\to}}\mathcal{L}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C}}^{n}.

Let us consider a fine resolution for this complex. Let
\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{L})
be the sheaf of
\mathcal{L}
-valued smooth
k
-forms on
\mathcal{X}
. By our construction, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{L})=p_{2}^{*}dz\wedge p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{k-1}(X,L)\bigoplus p_{2}^{*}d\bar{z}\wedge p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{k-1}(X,L)\bigoplus p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{k}(X,L)

(12.3.2)

where we denote by

p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{k}(X,L):=p_{1}^{-1}\mathcal{A}^{k}(X,L)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}_{X}}\mathcal{C}^{\infty}\mathcal{X}.

We denote by

\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L}):=\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{L})/p_{2}^{*}d\bar{z}\wedge p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{k-1}(X,L)\bigoplus p_{1}^{*}\mathcal{A}^{k}(X,L).

Let

D^{\prime\prime}_{\omega}:\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L})

be the composition

\mathcal{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}}+\tilde{\omega}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{L})\to\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L}).

Note that
D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime 2}=0
. Hence we have another complex

\mathcal{A}^{0}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{1}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}}}{{\to}}\mathcal{A}^{2n}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L}).

Claim 12.4
.

The natural morphism
{\rm Dol}(\mathcal{L},\theta)\to(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L}),D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime})
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is exactly the same as
Proposition

11.9
. We omit it.
∎

Note that we have

\displaystyle{\rm Dol}(\mathcal{X},\theta)|_{X\times\{z\}}=L^{\bullet}_{zw}:=L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wedge z\omega}}{{\to}}L\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wedge z\omega}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wedge z\omega}}{{\to}}L\otimes\Omega_{X}^{n},

(12.4.1)

We simply write
L^{\bullet}
for
L^{\bullet}_{0}
.
By the definition, there exists
\omega\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
such that
{\rm codim}_{X}Z(w)=w(X)=:m
.
Fix any
i<m
. We shall prove that
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet})=0
for a general
L\in{\rm Pic}^{0}(X)
. We assume by contradiction that,
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet})\neq 0
for any
L\in{\rm Pic}^{0}(X)
. It follows that, for a general
L\in{\rm Pic}^{0}(X)
, the dimension of
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\prime\bullet})
is constant for line bundles
L^{\prime}
in a neighborhood of
L
in
\ {\rm Pic}^{0}(X)
. Therefore, by the same arguments as in the proof of
12.1
, for any
\eta\in A^{0,1}(X)
such that
\bar{\partial}\eta=0
, and any
u\in A^{p,q}(X,L)
with
p+q=i
,

\eta\wedge u
is
\bar{\partial}_{L}
-exact. Hence
the cup product

H^{p,q}(X,L)\otimes H^{0,1}(X)\to H^{p,q+1}(X,L)

vanishes identically. If we take the conjugate, then

\displaystyle H^{q,p}(X,L^{\star})\otimes H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\to H^{q+1,p}(X,L^{\star}),

(12.4.2)

vanishes identically, where
L^{\star}
is the dual of
L
.
In what follows, we replace
L
by
L^{\star}
for notational simplicity.

For any
e\in H^{p,q}(X,L)
, we choose a harmonic form
u_{0}\in\mathcal{H}^{p,q}(X,L)
representing
e
. Then by (
12.4.2
), we have

u_{0}\wedge\omega=\bar{\partial}_{L}v

for some
v\in A^{p+1,q-1}
. Let
\partial_{L}
be the
(1,0)
-part of the Chern connection
\nabla_{h}
for
(L,h)
. Note that
\nabla_{h}^{2}=0
. Then we have

\partial_{L}(u_{0}\wedge\omega)=0=\bar{\partial}_{L}(u_{0}\wedge\omega).

By the
\partial\bar{\partial}
-lemma, there exists some
\gamma_{1}\in A^{p,q-1}(X,L)
such that

u_{0}\wedge\omega=\bar{\partial}_{L}\partial^{\prime}_{L}\gamma_{1}.

Let
u_{1}:=-\partial^{\prime}_{L}\gamma_{1}
and we consider
u_{1}\wedge\omega
. It is also
\partial^{\prime}_{L}
-exact, and both
\partial_{L}^{\prime}
and
\bar{\partial}_{L}
closed. Therefore, by the
\partial\bar{\partial}
-lemma again, there exists some
\gamma_{2}\in A^{p,q-1}(X,L)
such that

u_{1}\wedge\omega=\bar{\partial}_{L}\partial^{\prime}_{L}\gamma_{2}

and we set
u_{2}:=-\partial^{\prime}_{L}\gamma_{2}
. We iterate this algorithm, and obtain
u_{1},\ldots,u_{k},\ldots,
such that for any
i\geqslant 0
,

\displaystyle u_{i}\wedge\omega=-\bar{\partial}_{L}u_{i+1}.

(12.4.3)

Based on the same arguments as in
10.22
, we can assume that there exists some
\varepsilon>0
such that

u:=\sum_{i\geqslant 0}z^{i}u_{i}\in A^{k}(\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\mathcal{L}),

where
\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}:=X\times\mathbb{D}_{\varepsilon}
. By (
12.4.3
), we have

(\bar{\partial}_{\mathcal{L}}+\tilde{\omega})\sigma=0.

Let
\sigma\in\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L}))
be the image of
u
under the quotient
\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{L})\to\mathcal{A}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{C},\mathcal{L})
. Then we have

D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}(\sigma)=0.

Note that we have

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Gamma(\mathbb{D}_{\varepsilon},\mathbb{R}^{i}p_{2,*}({\rm Dol}(\mathcal{X},\theta)))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{i}(\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon},{\rm Dol}(\mathcal{X},\theta))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}((A^{\bullet}(\mathcal{X}_{\varepsilon}/\mathbb{D}_{\varepsilon},\mathcal{L}),D_{\omega}^{\prime\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{i}(A^{\bullet}(X,L),\bar{\partial}_{L})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

where the vertical arrows are isomorphic. The above arguments imply that
\psi
is surjective. It follows that

\mathbb{R}^{i}p_{2,*}({\rm Dol}(\mathcal{X},\theta)(\mathbb{D}_{\varepsilon})\to\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet})

is surjective. By the cohomolgy and base change theorem, if we shrink
\varepsilon
,

z\mapsto\dim\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet}_{z\omega})
is constant for
z\in\mathbb{D}_{\varepsilon}
. Therefore, by the assumption,
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet}_{z\omega})\neq 0
. Note that
{\rm codim}_{X}Z(z\omega)={\rm codim}_{X}Z(\omega)>i
if
z\neq 0
. By
12.3
, we obtain that
\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,L^{\bullet}_{z\omega})=0
, which yields a contradiction. The theorem is thus proved.
∎
